paper/methods)\n   142→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   143→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   144→| Neurons (total) | Not directly reported | \"89 recordings in 19 mice\" + \"20,547 to 89,577 neurons in each recording\" |\n   145→| Neurons / session | 20,547 to 89,577 | \"20,547 to 89,577 neurons in each recording\" |\n   146→| Subjects | 19 | \"89 recordings in 19 mice\" |\n   147→| Sessions / subject | Not directly reported in text | \"each mouse can have more than one imaging session\" |\n   148→| Trials (total) | Not directly reported in text | Not directly stated |\n   149→| Trials / session | Not directly reported in text | Not directly stated |\n   150→| Neural data time bin | Framewise deconvolved traces; exact Hz not stated in paper/methods excerpt | \"All our analyses were based on deconvolved fluorescence traces\" |\n   151→| Behavior data time bin | Running speed interpolated at 0.1 m position bins for some analyses | \"0–6 m, with a 0.1-m step size\" |\n   152→| Reward rate | Approximately 50% by task design; one rewarded corridor of two | \"water in rewarded trials only\" |\n   153→| Corridor structure | 4 m texture corridor + 2 m grey space | \"corridors were each 4 m long, with 2 m of grey space\" |\n   154→| Cue position | Uniform 0.5 m to 3.5 m from corridor entry | \"uniform distribution between positions 0.5 m and 3.5 m\" |\n   155→| Reward zone / reward position | Begins at cue in imaging task; reward positions approx. uniform 0.5 m to 3.5 m | \"reward delivery locations were also approximately uniformly distributed between 0.5 m and 3.5 m\" |\n   156→| Running threshold | VR moves when speed exceeds 6 cm/s | \"running faster than a threshold of 6 cm s−1\" |\n   157→| Selective neuron threshold | d′ >= 0.3 or d′ <= -0.3 | \"d′ ≥ 0.3 or d′ ≤ −0.3\" |\n   158→| Coding-direction neuron selection | Top 5% selective neurons from train trials for each reference stimulus | \"top 5% selective neurons each\" |\n   159→\n   160→\n   161→### Processing Details\n   162→- Task structure:\n   163→  - Linear VR corridor with textures in the first `0–4 m` and grey space in `4–6 m`.\n   164→  - Trials are temporally aligned in the paper to corridor progression and, for reward-prediction analyses, additionally to sound cue and first lick.\n   165→  - Sound cue is present in all imaging trial types; for task mice it signals reward availability in the rewarded corridor.\n   166→- Neural signal:\n   167→  - Two-photon calcium imaging processed with Suite2p.\n   168→  - Analyses use deconvolved fluorescence traces, not raw fluorescence and not freshly computed dF/F in this repository.\n   169→- Core inclusion rule used throughout the paper:\n   170→  - \"We only considered timepoints during running for analysis.\"\n   171→  - For selectivity/coding analyses, only the texture area (`0–4 m`) is used for d-prime calculations.\n   172→- Sequence similarity analysis:\n   173→  - Use half of leaf1/circle1 trials as train trials to compute d′ and select neurons.\n   174→  - Use the other half as test trials, then split test trials into odd/even to compute preferred positions and correlations.\n   175→- Coding direction / similarity index:\n   176→  - Select leaf1- and circle1-selective neurons from train trials using the top 5% d′ tails.\n   177→  - Interpolate neural activity by position.\n   178→  - Normalize each neuron by subtracting grey-space baseline and dividing by the average of leaf1/circle1 response standard deviations.\n   179→  - Evaluate coding direction only on held-out trials.\n   180→  - Average projections within the texture area (`0–4 m`) to obtain similarity indices.\n   181→- Reward-prediction analysis:\n   182→  - Interpolate neural activity into trial x position matrices.\n   183→  - Restrict to leaf1 trials and split into early-cue vs late-cue trials using sound cue position.\n   184→  - Use cue position rather than reward position because cue and reward are highly correlated in rewarded trials.\n   185→  - Use 10-fold cross-validation: 9 folds to identify reward-prediction neurons, 1 fold to evaluate held-out trial activity.\n   186→- Running-speed analysis:\n   187→  - Keep epochs with speed > `6 cm/s` for at least `66 ms`.\n   188→  - Interpolate running speed both to imaging-frame times and to position bins (`0–6 m`, step `0.1 m`).\n   189→\n   190→### Curation Steps\n   191→\n   192→**Neuron curation rules**:\n   193→- Upstream curation is handled by Suite2p: motion correction, ROI detection, cell classification, neuropil correction, spike deconvolution.\n   194→- For selectivity-based analyses, neurons are filtered by d′ thresholds (`>= 0.3` or `<= -0.3`) or by top-5% selectivity tails depending on analysis.\n   195→- Reward-prediction neuron selection uses `d′late vs early >= 0.3`.\n   196→- Area-based analyses aggregate neurons into `V1`, medial, lateral, and anterior visual regions.\n   197→\n   198→**Trial curation rules**:\n   199→- Running-only timepoints are analyzed; non-running periods are excluded.\n   200→- Texture-based selectivity uses only positions within `0–4 m`.\n   201→- Sequence/coding analyses use held-out trials, with explicit train/test and odd/even splits.\n   202→- First-lick-aligned reward-prediction analysis includes only rewarded leaf1 trials with first lick later than `2 m` from corridor entry.\n   203→- Leaf2 lick/no-lick comparison excludes a mouse with only one no-lick leaf2 trial.\n   204→\n   205→### Decoders Trained\n   206→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   207→| No directly comparable generic decoder reported | The paper does not report balanced/classification accuracy for the custom decoder task in this project |\n   208→| Visual category separation (coding direction / similarity index) | Strong separation reported qualitatively; not reported as classifier accuracy |\n   209→| Reward prediction (late vs early cue leaf1 trials) | Identified via `d′late vs early`, not reported as classifier accuracy |\n   210→\n   211→---\n   212→\n   213→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   214→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   215→\n   216→### Discrepancies Found\n   217→| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n   218→|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n   219→| Recording count | Notebook iterates all `exp_info` sessions | `89` unique `spk/*_neural_data.npy` sessions | \"89 recordings in 19 mice\" | Consistent. Use all 89 unique imaging sessions indexed by `Imaging_Exp_info.npy`. |\n   220→| Subject count | `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` contains repeated mouse IDs across experiments | `19` unique `mname` values | \"19 mice\" | Consistent. Use `mname` as subject ID. |\n   221→| Corridor geometry / units | Code uses `n_bins=60`, texture area `:40`, previous/grey bins near `40:60` | `Corridor_Length=60`, `Texture_Length=40`, `Gray_Space_length=20`, `run_pos.shape=(ntrials, 60)` | \"4 m long, with 2 m of grey space\" | Consistent after unit conversion: raw data are in decimeters over a 6 m total corridor. |\n   222→| Running-only analysis | Code uses `ft_move > 0` and `ft_CorrSpc` masks | Behavior files contain `ft_move`, `ft_CorrSpc`, `ft_GraySpc`, `ft_RunSpeed` | \"We only considered timepoints during running\" | Consistent. Conversion should restrict frame-based analyses to running periods when mirroring reference logic. |\n   223→| Trial parity naming | Code comments say \"odd trials\" but uses `ft_trInd % 2 == 0` and `trInd % 2 == 1` | `trInd_odd` is `True` on raw trial indices `0,2,4,...` | Paper says train/test split with odd/even trials | Resolved as a naming convention issue: arrays are zero-based, so \"odd trials\" in the paper/comments means 1st/3rd/5th... trials. Mirror the exact boolean logic from the reference code, not the literal wording. |\n   224→| Cue position field | Code sometimes uses `SoundPos`; reward-prediction code often uses `np.mod(SoundDelPos, 60)` | Raw `SoundDelPos` can be cumulative and exceed corridor length; modulo-60 yields within-corridor positions. `SoundPos` and `RewPos` are also within-corridor scales. | Paper describes cue positions within the corridor | Resolved by following the reference code: treat cue/reward position variables in within-corridor coordinates, using modulo corridor length where needed. |\n   225→| Cue-position range | Raw data ranges are slightly broader than the simplified paper wording (`SoundPos` roughly `1.1` to `39.0` dm; `RewPos` up to `43.8` dm) | Empirical ranges extend a bit beyond the paper’s idealized `0.5–3.5 m` statement | Paper says cue positions were uniform between `0.5 m` and `3.5 m` | Minor discrepancy likely from delayed-cue / passive-reward session details and sampling noise. Keep raw within-corridor values from data, not clipped values. |\n   226→| Swap stimuli handling | Code keeps `leaf1_swap1` and `leaf1_swap2` as separate IDs/files | `test3` behavior keys are split into `_swap1` / `_swap2` sessions | Paper says swap types are pooled for statistics | Resolve by preserving raw swap subtype identity in converted trial labels; pooling, if needed, should happen only in downstream statistics. |\n   227→| Behavior-only versus imaging data | Code notebook centers on `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` and matching `spk/` + `retinotopy/` | `data/beh/example_bef_and_aft_learning_behavior.npy` exists without matching neural files | Paper includes both imaging and behavior-only experiments | For the decoder conversion, use only imaging sessions that have matched neural and retinotopy data. |\n   228→\n   229→---\n   230→\n   231→## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n   232→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   233→\n   234→### Variable Mapping\n   235→| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n   236→|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n   237→| `spk/*_neural_data.npy` -> `['spks']` via `utils.load_spk()` | `neural` | Load deconvolved traces; slice per unique session and trial using frame indices between `StartFr` and `GrayFr`; retain only running frames (`ft_move > 0`) within the texture area; keep curated neurons only | `load_spk`, `Get_dprime_selective_neuron`, `Get_coding_direction`, `get_kfold_reward_response` | Running-only and texture-only follows the paper; `GrayFr` end keeps exactly the 0–4 m texture interval. |\n   238→| `retinotopy/*_trans.npz` -> `iarea` | `brain_region_idx` | Map neurons to `V1`, `mHV`, `lHV`, `aHV`, else `other` | `load_retino`, `neu_area_ID` | Preserve all curated neurons and record their broad visual-region label. |\n   239→| Session key / recording date | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | `subjects = sorted(unique mname)`; `subject_idx` indexes subject per session | `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` | Sessions are deduplicated by base session ID `<mouse>_<date>_<blk>`. |\n   240→| `beh['SoundTime']`, `beh['ft']`, `StartFr:GrayFr` running frames | `input[0]` = `time_to_sound_cue_sec` | For each retained frame, `(SoundTime[trial] - ft[frame]) * 86400`; can be positive before cue and negative after cue | Paper Methods, `get_kfold_reward_response`, `spk_2_cue` | Continuous time-varying input in seconds. |\n   241→| Session recording date / optional `db['days']` | `input[1]` = `day_of_training` | Use elapsed calendar days since the mouse’s first recording date; repeat across retained frames in the trial | `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` | Chosen because a complete per-session training-day label is not available for all sessions. |\n   242→| `beh['ft']`, `StartFr` | `input[2]` = `time_since_trial_start_sec` | `(ft[frame] - ft[StartFr[trial]]) * 86400` for each retained frame | Behavior frame timing fields | Continuous time-varying input in seconds. |\n   243→| `beh['isRew']` | `input[3]` = `reward_available` | Trialwise 0/1 repeated across retained frames | Behavior session dict | Uses rewarded-corridor identity even in unsupervised sessions, matching reference use of `isRew`. |\n   244→| `beh['WallName']` | `output[0]` = `visual_stimulus_category` | Map exact wall name to integer category; repeat across retained frames | Behavior session dict | Use `WallName` directly so swap sessions and non-leaf/circle stimuli are preserved correctly. |\n   245→| `beh['LickFr']`, `beh['LickTrind']` | `output[1]` = `licking` | Binary per retained frame: 1 if one or more licks occur on that imaging frame, else 0 | `spk_2_firstLick`, `spk_2_cue`, behavior docs | Time-varying categorical output. |\n   246→| `beh['ft_Pos']` on retained frames | `output[2]` = `position_bin` | Discretize `0–40 dm` into 4 equal bins: `[0,10), [10,20), [20,30), [30,40]` | Paper Methods (0–4 m texture area) | Exactly matches the requested 4 one-meter bins. |\n   247→| `beh['ft_RunSpeed']` on retained frames | `output[3]` = `running_speed_bin` | Compute global quartile edges over all retained running frames in all sessions; assign bin 0–3 | Paper running-speed interpolation + user decoder spec | Quartiles computed on the included running-only texture frames. |\n   248→\n   249→### Key Decisions\n   250→1. **Deduplicate 142 behavior entries down to 89 unique neural sessions**: The same recording appears under multiple analysis labels in `Imaging_Exp_info.npy`. Conversion will use one canonical copy per base session ID `<mouse>_<date>_<blk>`, preferring a non-`stimtype` entry when present. For `test3` swap sessions, either suffix copy is sufficient because `WallName` contains the true trial label and the framewise data are duplicated.\n   251→2. **Use the texture segment only (`StartFr:GrayFr`)**: The decoder output requires exactly four 1 m position bins. Ending trials at grey-space entry also matches the paper’s `0–4 m` texture-area analyses.\n   252→3. **Keep running frames only inside the texture segment**: This matches the reference paper/code rule that analyses use running timepoints and removes extremely long paused trials. Actual elapsed time is still preserved through the continuous time inputs.\n   253→4. **Curate neurons using paper-defined task relevance**: Keep neurons in retinotopically assigned visual regions that are either:\n   254→   - corridor-responsive and stimulus-selective with `|d′| >= 0.3` between the primary rewarded and non-rewarded exemplar-1 corridors, or\n   255→   - reward-prediction neurons with `d′late_vs_early >= 0.3` on the rewarded exemplar-1 corridor.\n   256→   This follows the two main neuron-selection motifs in the paper and keeps the conversion computationally tractable.\n   257→5. **Use exact `WallName` strings as stimulus labels**: This avoids ambiguity from `stim_id` NaNs in swap sessions and preserves non-leaf/circle texture pairs (`rock*`, `wood*`).\n   258→6. **Represent all decoder inputs as time-varying arrays**: Even trial-constant variables (`day_of_training`, `reward_available`) will be repeated across timepoints so the dataset is uniform and easy for `train_decoder.py`.\n   259→7. **Use actual frame times in seconds, not just frame index**: Because non-running frames are dropped, elapsed time inputs must come from `ft` timestamps rather than assuming contiguous native-frame spacing.\n   260→8. **Use day-since-first-recording as the training-day proxy**: The paper does not provide a complete per-session training-day label, while calendar date is available for every recording. The small subset of sessions with `db['days']` can be used later as a sanity check.\n   261→\n   262→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   263→- [ ] For a sampled session/trial/neuron, verify the converted neural trace matches the raw `spk` values at the retained running frames using `np.allclose()`.\n   264→- [ ] For sampled trials, verify `time_since_trial_start_sec + time_to_sound_cue_sec` equals the trial’s cue time-from-start at every retained frame.\n   265→- [ ] For sampled trials, verify licking output is exactly the binary projection of raw `LickFr` events onto retained imaging frames.\n   266→- [ ] For sampled trials, verify position bins come directly from raw `ft_Pos` thresholds at retained frames.\n   267→- [ ] For sampled trials, verify running-speed bins correspond to the stored global quartile edges applied to raw `ft_RunSpeed`.\n   268→- [ ] Verify session/subject/trial counts after deduplication equal `89` sessions, `19` subjects, and `38,110` trials.\n   269→\n this indicates the dataset used in the paper is calcium imaging, not electrophysiology.\n- The reference code does **not** compute delta-F-over-F inside this repository. `load_spk()` directly loads `*_neural_data.npy` and concatenates `['spks']`, so the saved neural signal is already the processed neural activity used downstream.\n- The canonical spatial representation is `60` bins across a `6 m` corridor, i.e. `1 dm` spatial bins (`data_process_script.ipynb`, cell 9).\n- Interpolation uses only frames with `ft_move > 0` (mouse moving). The notebook saves `process_data/*_interpolate_spk.npy` produced by `utils.get_interpPos_spk(spk[:, VRmove], ft_AcumPos[VRmove], ntrials, n_bins=60, lengths=CL)`.\n- Stimulus selectivity / coding analyses use moving corridor frames only: `corr_fr = beh['ft_CorrSpc'][:nfr] & (beh['ft_move'][:nfr] > 0)`.\n- Trial splitting in the reference code is by parity of trial index:\n  - `ft_trInd % 2 == 0` is used as the \"odd trials\" training split in comments/code.\n  - `trInd % 2 == 1` is used as the held-out/even split for projection or sorting outputs.\n- `Get_coding_direction()` performs a specific normalization on interpolated activity:\n  - keep texture area `:40` bins,\n  - compute gray baseline from bins `42:52`,\n  - normalize by combined std from reference stimulus trials,\n  - prepend `n_bef=10` bins from the previous trial gray segment.\n- Neuron curation is analysis-specific rather than a universal dataset filter:\n  - imaging session\" |\n| Trials (total) | Not directly reported in text | Not directly stated |\n| Trials / session | Not directly reported in text | Not directly stated |\n| Neural data time bin | Framewise deconvolved traces; exact Hz not stated in paper/methods excerpt | \"All our analyses were based on deconvolved fluorescence traces\" |\n| Behavior data time bin | Running speed interpolated at 0.1 m position bins for some analyses | \"0–6 m, with a 0.1-m step size\" |\n| Reward rate | Approximately 50% by task design; one rewarded corridor of two | \"water in rewarded trials only\" |\n| Corridor structure | 4 m texture corridor + 2 m grey space | \"corridors were each 4 m long, with 2 m of grey space\" |\n| Cue position | Uniform 0.5 m to 3.5 m from corridor entry | \"uniform distribution between positions 0.5 m and 3.5 m\" |\n| Reward zone / reward position | Begins at cue in imaging task; reward positions approx. uniform 0.5 m to 3.5 m | \"reward delivery locations were also approximately uniformly distributed between 0.5 m and 3.5 m\" |\n| Running threshold | VR moves when speed exceeds 6 cm/s | \"running faster than a threshold of 6 cm s−1\" |\n| Selective neuron threshold | d′ >= 0.3 or d′ <= -0.3 | \"d′ ≥ 0.3 or d′ ≤ −0.3\" |\n| Coding-direction neuron selection | Top 5% selective neurons from train trials for each reference stimulus | \"top 5% selective neurons each\" |\n\n\n### Processing Details\n- Task structure:\n  - Linear VR corridor with textures in the first `0–4 m` and grey space in `4–6 m`.\n  - Trials are temporally aligned in the paper to corridor progression and, for reward-prediction analyses, additionally to sound cue and first lick.\n  - Sound cue is present in all imaging trial types; for task mice it signals reward availability in the rewarded corridor.\n- Neural signal:\n  - Two-photon calcium imaging processed with Suite2p.\n  - Analyses use deconvolved fluorescence traces, not raw fluorescence and not freshly computed dF/F in this repository.\n- Core inclusion rule used throughout the paper:\n  - \"We only considered timepoints during running for analysis.\"\n  - For selectivity/coding analyses, only the texture area (`0–4 m`) is used for d-prime calculations.\n- Sequence similarity analysis:\n  - Use half of leaf1/circle1 trials as train trials to compute d′ and select neurons.\n  - Use the other half as test trials, then split test trials into odd/even to compute preferred positions and correlations.\n- Coding direction / similarity index:\n  - Select leaf1- and circle1-selective neurons from train trials using the top 5% d′ tails.\n  - Interpolate neural activity by position.\n  - Normalize each neuron by subtracting grey-space baseline and dividing by the average of leaf1/circle1 response standard deviations.\n  - Evaluate coding direction only on held-out trials.\n  - Average projections within the texture area (`0–4 m`) to obtain similarity indices.\n- Reward-prediction analysis:\n  - Interpolate neural activity into trial x position matrices.\n  - Restrict to leaf1 trials and split into early-cue vs late-cue trials using sound cue position.\n  - Use cue position rather than reward position because cue and reward are highly correlated in rewarded trials.\n  - Use 10-fold cross-validation: 9 folds to identify reward-prediction neurons, 1 fold to evaluate held-out trial activity.\n- Running-speed analysis:\n  - Keep epochs with speed > `6 cm/s` for at least `66 ms`.\n  - Interpolate running speed both to imaging-frame times and to position bins (`0–6 m`, step `0.1 m`).\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules**:\n- Upstream curation is handled by Suite2p: motion correction, ROI detection, cell classification, neuropil correction, spike deconvolution.\n- For selectivity-based analyses, neurons are filtered by d′ thresholds (`>= 0.3` or `<= -0.3`) or by top-5% selectivity tails depending on analysis.\n- Reward-prediction neuron selection uses `d′late vs early >= 0.3`.\n- Area-based analyses aggregate neurons into `V1`, medial, lateral, and anterior visual regions.\n\n**Trial curation rules**:\n- Running-only timepoints are analyzed; non-running periods are excluded.\n- Texture-based selectivity uses only positions within `0–4 m`.\n- Sequence/coding analyses use held-out trials, with explicit train/test and odd/even splits.\n- First-lick-aligned reward-prediction analysis includes only rewarded leaf1 trials with first lick later than `2 m` from corridor entry.\n- Leaf2 lick/no-lick comparison excludes a mouse with only one no-lick leaf2 trial.\n\n### Decoders Trained\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n| No directly comparable generic decoder reported | The paper does not report balanced/classification accuracy for the custom decoder task in this project |\n| Visual category separation (coding direction / similarity index) | Strong separation reported qualitatively; not reported as classifier accuracy |\n| Reward prediction (late vs early cue leaf1 trials) | Identified via `d′late vs early`, not reported as classifier accuracy |\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Discrepancies Found\n| Topic | Code says | Data shows | Paper says | Resolution |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Recording count | Notebook iterates all `exp_info` sessions | `89` unique `spk/*_neural_data.npy` sessions | \"89 recordings in 19 mice\" | Consistent. Use all 89 unique imaging sessions indexed by `Imaging_Exp_info.npy`. |\n| Subject count | `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` contains repeated mouse IDs across experiments | `19` unique `mname` values | \"19 mice\" | Consistent. Use `mname` as subject ID. |\n| Corridor geometry / units | Code uses `n_bins=60`, texture area `:40`, previous/grey bins near `40:60` | `Corridor_Length=60`, `Texture_Length=40`, `Gray_Space_length=20`, `run_pos.shape=(ntrials, 60)` | \"4 m long, with 2 m of grey space\" | Consistent after unit conversion: raw data are in decimeters over a 6 m total corridor. |\n| Running-only analysis | Code uses `ft_move > 0` and `ft_CorrSpc` masks | Behavior files contain `ft_move`, `ft_CorrSpc`, `ft_GraySpc`, `ft_RunSpeed` | \"We only considered timepoints during running\" | Consistent. Conversion should restrict frame-based analyses to running periods when mirroring reference logic. |\n| Trial parity naming | Code comments say \"odd trials\" but uses `ft_trInd % 2 == 0` and `trInd % 2 == 1` | `trInd_odd` is `True` on raw trial indices `0,2,4,...` | Paper says train/test split with odd/even trials | Resolved as a naming convention issue: arrays are zero-based, so \"odd trials\" in the paper/comments means 1st/3rd/5th... trials. Mirror the exact boolean logic from the reference code, not the literal wording. |\n| Cue position field | Code sometimes uses `SoundPos`; reward-prediction code often uses `np.mod(SoundDelPos, 60)` | Raw `SoundDelPos` can be cumulative and exceed corridor length; modulo-60 yields within-corridor positions. `SoundPos` and `RewPos` are also within-corridor scales. | Paper describes cue positions within the corridor | Resolved by following the reference code: treat cue/reward position variables in within-corridor coordinates, using modulo corridor length where needed. |\n| Cue-position range | Raw data ranges are slightly broader than the simplified paper wording (`SoundPos` roughly `1.1` to `39.0` dm; `RewPos` up to `43.8` dm) | Empirical ranges extend a bit beyond the paper’s idealized `0.5–3.5 m` statement | Paper says cue positions were uniform between `0.5 m` and `3.5 m` | Minor discrepancy likely from delayed-cue / passive-reward session details and sampling noise. Keep raw within-corridor values from data, not clipped values. |\n| Swap stimuli handling | Code keeps `leaf1_swap1` and `leaf1_swap2` as separate IDs/files | `test3` behavior keys are split into `_swap1` / `_swap2` sessions | Paper says swap types are pooled for statistics | Resolve by preserving raw swap subtype identity in converted trial labels; pooling, if needed, should happen only in downstream statistics. |\n| Behavior-only versus imaging data | Code notebook centers on `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` and matching `spk/` + `retinotopy/` | `data/beh/example_bef_and_aft_learning_behavior.npy` exists without matching neural files | Paper includes both imaging and behavior-only experiments | For the decoder conversion, use only imaging sessions that have matched neural and retinotopy data. |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `spk/*_neural_data.npy` -> `['spks']` via `utils.load_spk()` | `neural` | Load deconvolved traces; slice per unique session and trial using frame indices between `StartFr` and `GrayFr`; retain only running frames (`ft_move > 0`) within the texture area; keep curated neurons only | `load_spk`, `Get_dprime_selective_neuron`, `Get_coding_direction`, `get_kfold_reward_response` | Running-only and texture-only follows the paper; `GrayFr` end keeps exactly the 0–4 m texture interval. |\n| `retinotopy/*_trans.npz` -> `iarea` | `brain_region_idx` | Map neurons to `V1`, `mHV`, `lHV`, `aHV`, else `other` | `load_retino`, `neu_area_ID` | Preserve all curated neurons and record their broad visual-region label. |\n| Session key / recording date | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | `subjects = sorted(unique mname)`; `subject_idx` indexes subject per session | `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` | Sessions are deduplicated by base session ID `<mouse>_<date>_<blk>`. |\n| `beh['SoundTime']`, `beh['ft']`, `StartFr:GrayFr` running frames | `input[0]` = `time_to_sound_cue_sec` | For each retained frame, `(SoundTime[trial] - ft[frame]) * 86400`; can be positive before cue and negative after cue | Paper Methods, `get_kfold_reward_response`, `spk_2_cue` | Continuous time-varying input in seconds. |\n| Session recording date / optional `db['days']` | `input[1]` = `day_of_training` | Use elapsed calendar days since the mouse’s first recording date; repeat across retained frames in the trial | `Imaging_Exp_info.npy` | Chosen because a complete per-session training-day label is not available for all sessions. |\n| `beh['ft']`, `StartFr` | `input[2]` = `time_since_trial_start_sec` | `(ft[frame] - ft[StartFr[trial]]) * 86400` for each retained frame | Behavior frame timing fields | Continuous time-varying input in seconds. |\n| `beh['isRew']` | `input[3]` = `reward_available` | Trialwise 0/1 repeated across retained frames | Behavior session dict | Uses rewarded-corridor identity even in unsupervised sessions, matching reference use of `isRew`. |\n| `beh['WallName']` | `output[0]` = `visual_stimulus_category` | Map exact wall name to integer category; repeat across retained frames | Behavior session dict | Use `WallName` directly so swap sessions and non-leaf/circle stimuli are preserved correctly. |\n| `beh['LickFr']`, `beh['LickTrind']` | `output[1]` = `licking` | Binary per retained frame: 1 if one or more licks occur on that imaging frame, else 0 | `spk_2_firstLick`, `spk_2_cue`, behavior docs | Time-varying categorical output. |\n| `beh['ft_Pos']` on retained frames | `output[2]` = `position_bin` | Discretize `0–40 dm` into 4 equal bins: `[0,10), [10,20), [20,30), [30,40]` | Paper Methods (0–4 m texture area) | Exactly matches the requested 4 one-meter bins. |\n| `beh['ft_RunSpeed']` on retained frames | `output[3]` = `running_speed_bin` | Compute global quartile edges over all retained running frames in all sessions; assign bin 0–3 | Paper running-speed interpolation + user decoder spec | Quartiles computed on the included running-only texture frames. |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Deduplicate 142 behavior entries down to 89 unique neural sessions**: The same recording appears under multiple analysis labels in `Imaging_Exp_info.npy`. Conversion will use one canonical copy per base session ID `<mouse>_<date>_<blk>`, preferring a non-`stimtype` entry when present. For `test3` swap sessions, either suffix copy is sufficient because `WallName` contains the true trial label and the framewise data are duplicated.\n2. **Use the texture segment only (`StartFr:GrayFr`)**: The decoder output requires exactly four 1 m position bins. Ending trials at grey-space entry also matches the paper’s `0–4 m` texture-area analyses.\n3. **Keep running frames only inside the texture segment**: This matches the reference paper/code rule that analyses use running timepoints and removes extremely long paused trials. Actual elapsed time is still preserved through the continuous time inputs.\n4. **Curate neurons using paper-defined task relevance**: Keep neurons in retinotopically assigned visual regions that are either:\n   - corridor-responsive and stimulus-selective with `|d′| >= 0.3` between the primary rewarded and non-rewarded exemplar-1 corridors, or\n   - reward-prediction neurons with `d′late_vs_early >= 0.3` on the rewarded exemplar-1 corridor.\n   This follows the two main neuron-selection motifs in the paper and keeps the conversion computationally tractable.\n5. **Use exact `WallName` strings as stimulus labels**: This avoids ambiguity from `stim_id` NaNs in swap sessions and preserves non-leaf/circle texture pairs (`rock*`, `wood*`).\n6. **Represent all decoder inputs as time-varying arrays**: Even trial-constant variables (`day_of_training`, `reward_available`) will be repeated across timepoints so the dataset is uniform and easy for `train_decoder.py`.\n7. **Use actual frame times in seconds, not just frame index**: Because non-running frames are dropped, elapsed time inputs must come from `ft` timestamps rather than assuming contiguous native-frame spacing.\n8. **Use day-since-first-recording as the training-day proxy**: The paper does not provide a complete per-session training-day label, while calendar date is available for every recording. The small subset of sessions with `db['days']` can be used later as a sanity check.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [ ] For a sampled session/trial/neuron, verify the converted neural trace matches the raw `spk` values at the retained running frames using `np.allclose()`.\n- [ ] For sampled trials, verify `time_since_trial_start_sec + time_to_sound_cue_sec` equals the trial’s cue time-from-start at every retained frame.\n- [ ] For sampled trials, verify licking output is exactly the binary projection of raw `LickFr` events onto retained imaging frames.\n- [ ] For sampled trials, verify position bins come directly from raw `ft_Pos` thresholds at retained frames.\n- [ ] For sampled trials, verify running-speed bins correspond to the stored global quartile edges applied to raw `ft_RunSpeed`.\n- [ ] Verify session/subject/trial counts after deduplication equal `89` sessions, `19` subjects, and `38,110` trials.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**:
